<note>The illustration on this page depicts a symbolic alchemical landscape. In the heavens, the Sun and Moon (representing the dual masculine and feminine principles) watch over the process. A glass vessel—a <term>retort<gloss>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation.</gloss></term>—floats in the center, containing a black bird standing on a skull. This represents the stage of <term>Nigredo<gloss>The "blackening" or putrefaction stage of alchemy, where matter is broken down and "dies" before it can be reborn.</gloss></term>. Below, a pit of fire awaits the materials for further refinement.</note>

->A new offspring is sent down from high heaven<-
<note>original: "Nova progenies coelo demittitur alto." This is a quote from the Roman poet Virgil’s *Eclogues*. In an alchemical context, it suggests that the "Philosopher’s Stone" or the transformed soul is a divine gift descending into the material world.</note>

->O God, breathe favor upon my undertakings<-
<note>original: "Dei coeptis adspirate meis." A common invocation for divine help, adapted from the Roman poet Ovid. It emphasizes that the alchemist cannot succeed through physical labor alone, but requires the grace of God.</note>

->From the creation of the Distillation of the world<-
<note>original: "Ex creatione Distillationis mundi." Alchemists often viewed the creation of the universe as the first and greatest chemical experiment.</note>
->the dead head left behind by the will<-
<note>original: "relictum caput mortuum voluntate." The <term>caput mortuum<gloss>Literally "dead head"; the useless solid residue or dregs left over after a chemical reaction like distillation.</gloss></term> symbolizes the discarded physical shell or the impurities that must be removed.</note>
->of the Divine, to be calcined<-
<note>original: "Divina calcinandum." <term>Calcination<gloss>The process of heating a substance to a high temperature to burn away volatile parts or reduce it to a powder.</gloss></term> In spiritual alchemy, this represents the "burning away" of the ego or worldly attachments through the fire of divine discipline.</note>

<summary>This illustrated page depicts the beginning of the alchemical process, focusing on the concepts of Nigredo (decay) and Calcination (purification by fire). The Latin inscriptions frame the laboratory work as a sacred repetition of the world's creation, guided by divine will.</summary>

---

The full title of this very rare treatise—which fits exceedingly well into the current <term>Rosicrucian system<note>A mystical and philosophical movement of the 17th and 18th centuries that combined alchemy, Kabbalah, and Christian mysticism to seek a "universal reformation" of humanity.</note></term>—reads as follows:

> Microcosmic <term>Microcosmic<note>Referring to the "microcosm" or "little world." In this philosophy, the human being is viewed as a miniature version of the entire universe (the macrocosm), containing all the elements of the stars and the earth within themselves.</note></term> Preludes of the New Heaven and the New Earth; how it is permitted by God for Man, as the Image of God, [to bring forth] out of the old cursed earth a new [one] from the

These refer to the fundamental spiritual forces of the universe, often identified in this period with the "Two Foundations" of Boehmean philosophy.</note></term> working within outer, visible nature—namely, Light and Darkness—for the glorification of the great <term>Jehovah</term>, presented to the eyes of the world by a lover of divine and natural mysteries. Amsterdam, in the Year 1733, in <term>octavo<note>original: "8vo." A book size where the printing sheets are folded to create eight leaves, resulting in a medium-sized volume.</note></term>.

# of the Publisher.

bookseller, **Johann Andreas Rüdiger**, appeared. It is extremely rare, but also of no real value, because the anonymous <term>theosophical<note>Referring to "theosophy," a mystical philosophy seeking direct knowledge of God and the nature of the universe, popularized in this era by figures like Jacob Böhme.</note></term> author arranged a second edition ten years later. This second edition carries the same lengthy title but differs from the first in that, besides the <term>vermilion title<note>original: "Zinnobertitel." A title page printed in bright red ink, usually made from cinnabar; a mark of a more expensive or significant edition.</note></term>, it contains a <term>hieroglyphic-hermetic copperplate<note>An engraving filled with symbolic and mystical imagery related to Hermeticism (the study of alchemy and ancient wisdom).</note></term>, and was greatly expanded and improved in many places by the author himself. For this very reason, it is to be far preferred over the first edition. 

Based on the expanded 1744 edition with the copperplate, this current new reprint was organized by request. The shortening of the title was done merely because overcrowded or <term>densely typeset<note>original: "compreß gesetzt." Referring to a layout where text is tightly packed with little white space, a style that was becoming unfashionable in the mid-18th century.</note></term> titles are no longer pleasing at all. The errors that the first copper engraver had made in the Latin words found on the plate <note>original: "lateinischen Worten"</note> have been corrected in the new engraving. The faulty

<meta>This page continues the discussion of typographical errors found in previous editions and describes the third edition published in Danzig in 1755, emphasizing the necessity of the missing illustrations.</meta>

words read: *atto* instead of *alto* <note>Latin: "high"</note>; *reuctum* instead of *relictum* <note>Latin: "left behind"</note>; *cultinandum* instead of *calcinandum* <note>Latin: "to be calcined," referring to <term>calcination</term>, the alchemical process of heating a substance to high temperatures to effect a chemical change</note>. The third edition received the self-made title:

->**The Serpent of Moses, which devours all others, or never-before discovered chemical secrets, which represent the transformation of the old, cursed earth.**<-

Danzig, by Johann Christian Schuster, 1755, in <term>octavo<note>original: "in 8." A book size where the printer's sheet is folded into eight leaves, resulting in a medium-sized book.</note></term>. A mere reprint of the second edition, and moreover without the <term>copperplate<note>original: "Kupfer." An engraved illustration; in hermetic texts, these often contain symbolic keys to the philosophy that are not explained in the text.</note></term>.

Either the publisher did not want to incur the costs of the engraving, or he had provided a copy for printing that was missing the plate. The text without the illustration is not a complete whole. Organizing reprints is no great feat, but providing something complete is a primary concern with this kind of writing. Sometimes the copperplates suggest more than all the pages of text combined.

### From the Publisher.

<sig>A 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: bought (kaufte),</meta>

I could cite a great number of writings that have been reprinted with the <term>copperplates<note>original: "Kupfern." Engraved illustrations essential to alchemical and hermetic texts.</note></term> omitted. For example, the bookseller Vierling in Hof had an old German translation of the treatise: *Eugenius Philalethes’s Light from Light* <note>original: "Eugenii Philalethae lumen de lumine," a work by the Welsh alchemist Thomas Vaughan</note>, Hamburg 1693, in <term>octavo</term>, printed without bothering with the accompanying copperplate, which is <term>hieroglyphic<note>In 17th and 18th-century hermetic literature, "hieroglyphic" refers to a symbolic, allegorical diagram intended to be "read" by the initiated, rather than Egyptian script.</note></term> and bears the title: *The Figure of the Magic Schools* <note>original: "Scholae magicae typus"</note>. <term>Philalethes<note>The pseudonym of Thomas Vaughan (1621–1666), a prominent alchemist and mystic.</note></term> had not had it engraved for the English edition without good reason. This, however, is merely an aside.

Now, back to the *Microcosmic Preludes* <note>original: "microcosmischen Vorspielen"</note>. In all likelihood, the manuscript had been sent to the publisher without the author's name. This piqued his curiosity, and to satisfy it, <term>Dippel<note>Johann Konrad Dippel (1673–1734), a famous and controversial Pietist theologian and alchemist who wrote under the name Christianus Democritus.</note></term>—or *Christian Democritus*—who had become well-known through his writings at that time, was assumed to be the author. Rüdiger brought the treatise to the <term>Leipzig Easter Fair<note>The premier event for the German book trade, where new titles were debuted and distributed across Europe.</note></term>. Dippel, who [regularly] bought such writings—

<summary>The publisher highlights the importance of symbolic illustrations in alchemical texts and explains how the "Microcosmic Preludes" was anonymously submitted and initially misattributed to the famous alchemist Johann Konrad Dippel.</summary>

<keywords>Eugenius Philalethes, Thomas Vaughan, Johann Konrad Dippel, Christian Democritus, Leipzig Easter Fair, copperplate engravings, hieroglyphic diagrams, book trade history</keywords>

...[marketed] it, and sought to place his own manuscripts, he also came to Rüdiger, who told him straight to his face that he was the author of the *Microcosmic Preludes* <note>original: "microcosmichen Vorspiele."</note>. Dippel—who, upon reading them, immediately recognized the author and, in his proud imagination, believed the writing was directed against his own principles—flew into such a passion over it that he immediately wrote a refutation under the title: **Christian Democritus <note>The Latinized pseudonym used by Johann Konrad Dippel (1673–1734), an influential alchemist and theologian.</note>, a Sincere Protestant against a Writing <note>original: "Scriptum."</note> Falsely Attributed to Him at the Past Leipzig Easter Fair <note>original: "Jubilatemesse." A major international trade fair where new books were traditionally debuted.</note>. And an Orthodox Annihilator <note>A "destroyer" or one who reduces a thing to nothingness.</note> or Destroyer of the So-Called Microcosmic New Creation—likewise built from nothing in this Writing <note>original: "Scripto."</note>—as a Prelude to a New Heaven and a New Earth** etc. Written and also printed in the month of July 1733, in <term>octavo<note>A book size where the printing sheet is folded three times to create eight leaves (sixteen pages).</note></term>, 7 1/2 <term>sheets<note>original: "Bogen." In the early modern era, the length of a book was measured by the number of physical sheets of paper required for its printing.</note></term>.

### of the Publisher.

Immediately in the preface <note>original: "Vorberichte"</note>, it is stated that the author of the *Microcosmic* Preludes <note>original: "microcosmischen Vorſpiele"</note> was a Swiss man who afterwards established himself in Germany through a marriage he made with a person of high rank <note>A member of the nobility or aristocracy.</note>. However, just as Dippel was unable to refute this deep-searching Swiss man—whom he claimed to have known more than 30 years prior—with irrefutable arguments, so too does his flighty <note>Or "unsteady."</note> refutation deserve to be leafed through, because it contains several remarkable stories of metallic transformation <note>Alchemical transmutation, the changing of base metals into gold or silver.</note> that may still be unknown to many. Dippel possessed great knowledge in alchemy, as can be seen in the preface to his *Guide to Light and Right in External Nature* <note>original: "Wegweiſer zum Licht und Recht in der äußern Natur."</note>; he freely confesses that he found the <term>tincture<note>In alchemical literature, the "tincture" is the elusive substance—often the Philosopher's Stone in liquid form—used to perform transmutations.</note></term> from a chemical manuscript—which *Pierre-Jean Faber* <note>original: "Pet. Joh. Faber." A celebrated 17th-century French alchemist and physician to Louis XIII.</note> also published in Latin—and that everything succeeded for him without hindrance, just as if someone had guided him by the hand.

<sig>A 5</sig>
<meta>catchword: This (Dies)</meta>

<summary>The publisher identifies the author of the "Microcosmic Preludes" as a Swiss nobleman and discusses Johann Konrad Dippel's significant, though perhaps erratic, contributions to alchemy and his successful discovery of the "tincture."</summary>

## Preface of the Publisher.

This manuscript was published in Nuremberg in 1705 in a German translation, which has the following title: *The Brightly Shining Sun on the Alchemical Firmament of the High German Horizon* <note>original: "Die hellscheinende Sonne am alchymistischen Firmament des hochdeutschen Horizonts"</note>, that is, the manuscript of Dr. Pierre-Jean Faber <note>original: "Petri Ioh. Fabri"</note>, former personal physician to the French King. In Latin, it is found in: *Dr. Gabriel Clauder's Preliminary Treatise on the Universal Tincture, commonly called the Philosopher’s Stone, with P. J. Faber’s Manuscript explaining the obscure matters of the alchemists with extraordinary clarity, etc.* <note>original: "D. Gabrielis Clauderi Schediasm. de tinctura universali, vulgo lapis philosophorum dicta, cum P. I. Fabri Manuscripto res alchymicorum obscuras extraordinaria perspicuitate explanante &c."</note> Nuremberg 1736, in <term>quarto<note>A book size where each sheet of paper is folded twice to create four leaves or eight pages.</note></term>. This Latin edition is more frequently encountered than the German translation which was prepared by Dr. Horlacher. With this, the publisher recommends himself to the reader.

1783.

I. Y. R.

## Author's Preface.

---

There is a single thing in the nature of things <note>original: "in rerum natura"</note>; whoever possesses this possesses everything that is in heaven and on earth, but whoever does not have it has nothing, even if they were to possess all else. This single thing is a philosophical child <note>The "philosophical child" is a common alchemical metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone or the "Great Work" in its developing state.</note>, which is fathered by God and born through Nature. However, because it became outwardly very stained and impure during its first birth through corrupt nature, and therefore requires a rebirth from philosophical water and spirit—being completely incapable of restoring itself to such a state on its own—there must, during its rebir-

<vocab>Preface, philosophical child, rebirth, Nature, God, the nature of things</vocab>

<summary>The author introduces the "philosophical child" (the Philosopher's Stone) as a divine and natural creation that requires a spiritual and physical rebirth to reach its pure state.</summary>
<keywords>philosophical child, nature of things, rebirth, divine origin, alchemy, purification, philosophical water</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's preface, completing the word "rebirth" (Wiedergeburt) from the previous page's final sentence. The author explains why alchemical texts are often written in cryptic or symbolic language.</meta>

-th <note>Completing the word "rebirth" from the previous page.</note> the Philosopher must perform the services of a midwife, if it is to otherwise come into the world successfully. Once it is born, it gives to the one who helped it all heavenly and earthly pleasure in this world.

Thousands of books have been written about this child—how and where to obtain it—but very few of them are genuine and true. Among the ancients, who were true adepts <note>An "Adept" is a master who has successfully completed the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> of the philosophical child, one mostly finds the greatest sophistries <note>In this context, "sophistries" refers to intentionally confusing or deceptive language used to hide secrets.</note>, which has been attributed to those good people for many hundreds of years as a sign of envy <note>The "Envy of the Philosophers" (*invidia philosophorum*) was a standard excuse for why alchemical texts were written in code; it implies the authors were jealous of their secrets.</note>. However, one should know that they have been treated unfairly in this regard; they did not write this way out of envy, but for highly significant reasons, so that pearls would not be cast before swine. He who has open eyes will surely see the fruit beneath such leaves, and the roses among the thorns.

Others have written using nothing but hieroglyphs and figures without including practical processes, in order to lead the seekers of this divine being <note>original: "Entis". In alchemy, *Ens* refers to the fundamental essence or "entity" of a substance.</note> into the depths of God's creation, just as Ovid did in his *Metamorphoses*, and so forth. Still others write of the four elements and their transmutation of one into the other, as well as of the three principles <note>The "Three Principles" or *Tria Prima* are Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt, the foundational components of matter in Paracelsian alchemy.</note>, and how to cleanse them from their earthliness through proper solution <note>A "solution" in alchemy is the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state, often the first step in purification.</note>...

<meta>catchword: -fy (from the German "reinigen", meaning "to purify" or "to cleanse")</meta>

<summary>The author justifies the cryptic nature of alchemical writings as a necessary protection against the unworthy and outlines different symbolic approaches used by historical masters, such as Ovidian myth and the theory of the three principles.</summary>

<keywords>midwife metaphor, adepts, secrecy, pearls before swine, hieroglyphs, Ovid, four elements, three principles, purification, solution</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's preface. The first word "unite" completes the sentence from the previous page discussing the transmutation of elements.</meta>

->of the Author.<-

[to u]nite <note>Completing the word "vereinigen" (to unite) from the previous page.</note>, and must join together through the operation of the natural fire <term>natural fire|Ignis Naturae: In alchemy, this is a gentle, consistent heat—often compared to the warmth of a brooding hen—used to mature the "philosophical stone" over time.</term>. These authors are the best, and those whom I have followed. There is also a type of writer who either creates collections <note>original: "Collectanea"</note> from various philosophers and mixes their own mouse-droppings—that is, their chimeras and false processes—among the philosophical pepper; or else, in their blindness, they seek to interpret one or another true author who wrote from the light of God and of nature, in order to acquire the title of an Adept <term>Adept|A master who has successfully completed the "Great Work" of alchemy.</term> for themselves. Under such a name, they deceive the ignorant. One must guard oneself most carefully against these people and call upon God for His light, so that one is not led astray. 

The interpretation of philosophical writings proceeds in exactly the same way as the interpretation of the Bible. Whoever wishes to understand and interpret the latter must unfailingly possess the Spirit from which and in which it was written; and whoever wishes to understand the philosophers must possess that same light which shone for the philosophers. Where this is lacking, he will reason <note>original: "räsonniren"</note> about their writings just as a blind man speaks of color.

He can speak of great fortune whom God preserves from sophistry <note>In alchemical literature, "sophistry" refers to fraudulent recipes or deceptive logic that leads seekers into expensive, failed experiments.</note> and its books, or to whom He grants a friend to warn him and point him toward the right path. All those who do not [drink] from the well...

# Preface

[who have not] written from nature, but have instead begged together bits and pieces from processes <term>processes|Specific laboratory instructions or chemical procedures.</term> and other—though true—philosophical books, and have compiled them into a treatise <note>original: "Tractat"</note>, are deceivers. For even if they have often mixed in grand truths from the Hermetic writings <term>Hermetic writings|Refers to the "Hermetica," a body of secret wisdom and alchemical texts attributed to the legendary sage Hermes Trismegistus.</term>, these are nevertheless not true in their hands, because they have so stained and darkened them with their errors that the readers of such worthless rags <note>original: "Scharteken"</note>, who otherwise might still have had a little natural light, are completely blinded by them. It is a very certain sign that he who borrows from others has nothing of his own. He who understands the matter has no need at all to borrow; rather, nature dictates more to him than he can write.

When I once directed my mind toward Hermetic philosophy and obtained books that seemed to make the profound speeches of the philosophers quite clear through recipes <note>original: "Recepte"</note> and processes, I thought I already had the fish in the net <note>original: "Hamen" (a landing net)</note>, when I was not even yet at the water in which one must fish. It went with me as it does with all beginners, to whom the matter appears quite easy at first glance, but who finally—after gambling away their money and losing their time—realize that they have been deceived. I labored in common fire and with common species <term>common species|In alchemy, "common species" or "vulgar species" refer to ordinary, store-bought chemicals or metals (like common sulfur or mercury) as opposed to the "philosophical" substances refined by the artist.</term> no longer than a

# of the Author.

...which, by his own admission, he <term>worked on|original: "elaboriret"</term> in his early years, are so mixed together that hardly anyone can make sense of them; and this is because, due to a lack of understanding after his death (for during his life he published nothing), all his writings—the true along with the false—were <term>indiscriminately|original: "promiscue"</term> heaped together and shared with the public, to the great darkening of the <term>Light of Nature|a central alchemical concept referring to the innate wisdom and divine intelligence present in the natural world.</term>. His *Twelve Keys* and his <term>Book of Mining|original: "Bergbuch"; a work by Basil Valentine concerning minerals and metals.</term> are undoubtedly the best and most sincere; but because he believed he had revealed the truth too clearly in the first, he himself somewhat <term>obscured|original: "obscuriret"</term> it in the so-called *Explanation of the Twelve Keys* for the sake of the unworthy; thus, most people fall for such an explanation and thereby take a side path. It cannot be denied that fine medicine can be found here and there in his processes, but that is far removed from a <term>Tincture|original: "Tinctur"; the Elixir or Philosopher’s Stone, capable of transmuting metals and perfecting the human body.</term>, as the latter requires a completely different matter—unknown to the world—and a completely different fire and operation. Common <term>Chemists|original: "Chymici"</term> cook the water and other materials in the fire, but <term>Hermetic Philosophers|alchemists who follow the secret tradition of Hermes Trismegistus</term> cook the fire in the water; that is the difference and the entire secret, which consists in nothing other than <term>dissolving|original: "solviren"</term> and <term>coagulating|original: "coaguliren"</term>. <note>original: "solviren und coaguliren"; a reference to the famous alchemical maxim "Solve et Coagula," meaning to break down a substance to its essence and then reform it into a higher state.</note> Whoever can grasp it, let him grasp it.

# Preface

If it were not against my <term>intention|original: "Intent"</term> to be long-winded, this little treatise could have been greatly <term>extended|original: "extendiren"</term> without borrowing anything from others; however, I would have thereby increased and not diminished the <note>The author uses the truncated word "Verm-rung," likely "Verwirrung" (confusion), suggesting that longer texts often lead to more misunderstanding.</note> confusion in this divine <term>science|original: "Scienz"; here referring to the sacred art of alchemy</term>. Therefore, I have concentrated all breadth of detail, so as to place the honest lovers of divine and natural mysteries—through the reading of this work—at the <term>Center|original: "Centrum"</term> of their own minds, from which they can oversee the entire <term>periphery|original: "Peripherie"</term>. 

And certainly, whoever does not fundamentally understand the universal birth and destruction of things, which proceeds from the Center, should not venture to undertake anything in the <term>Hermetic Art|the tradition of alchemy and natural philosophy attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</term>, unless he wishes to experience harm to his body and the loss of his goods. I have clearly shown both <term>theoretically|original: "theoretice"</term> and <term>practically|original: "practice"</term> what is most important, and I have discovered and laid before everyone's eyes the <term>Master Key|original: "Hauptschlüssel"</term> to the <term>Royal Palace|a common alchemical metaphor for the completion of the Great Work or the state of perfected matter</term>, which is hidden by most practitioners. Thus, whoever follows my stated general positions and establishes himself firmly within them will, in time, surely find the benefit <term>in practice|original: "in praxi"</term>. 

In this writing, I have also striven for all possible clarity in order to light a lamp for those wandering in the great darkness. Since, however, I may not have sufficiently <term>explained|original: "expliciret"</term> myself on one point or another, or perhaps...

...should have forgotten anything, the extract included at the end from the *Great and Little Peasant* <note>The author refers to *Der Grosse und Kleine Bauer*, a well-known alchemical work by Johann Grasse (also known as Chortolasseus), published in the early 17th century.</note> will remedy such a deficiency.

The foundation of my philosophy is none other than the great cornerstone rejected by the builders—that is, by the wise men of Babel—which fills the whole world (Daniel 2:35); through and for whom all things are; through whom also everything in heaven and on earth is made, and in whom everything consists. From this universal foundation, the <term>Universal Medicine|a legendary alchemical substance capable of curing all diseases and perfecting both metal and the human soul</term>, of which I mainly treat here, must truly proceed if it is otherwise to be righteous. 

And because without this foundation, there can be no perfect contentment of the mind (even if one possessed the <term>Lapis|original: "Lapis"; the Philosopher's Stone</term>), I have endeavored to renovate this foundation—which among most Christians is corrupted with <term>chimeras|imaginary monsters or, in this context, false and fanciful ideas</term> and empty imaginings—and to bring its essential power and capacity back to light. I was particularly moved to this by the important article regarding the <term>Atonement of Christ|original: "satisfactione Christi"; the theological doctrine that Christ’s suffering and death satisfied the requirements of divine justice</term>, which has hitherto been contested for and against <note>original: "pro und contra"</note>. One party rejects it <note>original: "in totum"</note> entirely, while the other extends and abuses it too far, and thus both miss the proper aim. For I have observed that through the poor application of this article, many thousands of people are hindered from the essential knowledge of God and Christ and run into destruction.

# Preface of the Author.

Whoever might wish to judge or illuminate this writing must do so with no foreign light, but with the very same light that shines for me—namely, with the Light of God and of Nature. He must first thoroughly cleanse and polish this light of his from all prejudices; only then will I speak <note>The OCR "r-" likely represents "reden" (to speak).</note> with him, and I know for certain that the verdict <note>original: "Sentenz"; a formal judgment or opinion.</note> shall fall in my favor. It is indeed the height of fashion <note>original: "grande mode".</note> now that those who are called "learned" wish to judge immediately <note>original: "ex tempore"; without preparation.</note> everything that is beyond their horizon or sphere, and which they have not been able to think upon for even a single day in their lives. If it will not fit their measurement <note>original: "nach ihrer Elle", referring to an "ell," a traditional unit of length often used metaphorically for one's own standards.</note>, they reject it. Eternal Wisdom laughs and scoffs at them, and constantly closes her mysterious cabinet <note>original: "geheimnißvolles Cabinet"; a private room for study or the storage of curiosities.</note> before them, so that they can see nothing more than external things that come from the corrupted realm of nature.

Should some <term>tautologies|the redundant repetition of the same idea in different words</term> happen to occur in this little book, and the connection <note>original: "Connexion"; the logical flow or transition between ideas.</note> not be hit upon too accurately in some places, the reader is asked to kindly excuse this; I herewith commend him to the grace of the Most High. Given in my study <note>original: "Musäo"; from the Latin musaeum, referring to a scholar’s place of study or a library.</note>, November 10th, in the year 1732.

### §. 1.

The **Highest Good** <note>original: "Summum Bonum"; a Latin philosophical term referring to the ultimate goal of human existence.</note> or the highest **happiness** in this world, which all people seek and desire—if not openly, then secretly—consists in a complete **contentment of the mind** <note>original: "Zufriedenheit des Gemüths". The "Gemüth" refers to the totality of a person’s inner state, including thoughts, feelings, and soul.</note>. This mental contentment, however, if it is to exist or be, requires:

1) A healthy body.
2) That one does not **depend** <note>original: "dependire"; from the Latin 'dependere', meaning to hang from or be reliant upon.</note> on anyone, whether they be of high or low station, for one’s **subsistence** <term>subsistence|the means of supporting life; one's basic livelihood</term> and physical nourishment.
3) That one is assured of the **grace of God** through an essential knowledge and internal feeling, and thereby possesses a quiet **conscience** and a constant joy toward Him.

Whoever lacks one of these three pieces is still far removed from complete contentment of the mind, and thus from the highest happiness.

### §. 2.

A healthy body is a noble gift of God; however, if temporal nourishment... <note>The sentence ends abruptly here, suggesting it continues on the following page.</note>

### §. 3.

**Temporal goods** <note>original: "Zeitliche Güter"; refers to worldly wealth, possessions, and status that only last for a lifetime.</note> in the world carry a great reputation, and almost all eyes and hearts are focused upon them. However, when one considers how they are brought together nowadays, and that for their acquisition not only health but also often the **conscience** must be **neglected** <note>original: "postponiret"; from the Latin 'postponere', meaning to put behind or set aside.</note>, then once again a great **deficiency** <note>original: "Defect"; a lack or failure in completeness.</note> exists regarding the **contentment of the mind**.

### §. 4.

Some, though very few, do indeed have a quiet conscience and therefore a **joyfulness** <note>original: "Freudigkeit"; in this context, a sense of spiritual confidence or boldness before God.</note> toward God; but because they are often besieged either by the ailments of the body or by a lack of **nourishment**, their contentment of mind is frequently interrupted and disturbed.

### §. 5.

Now the question is, how should one begin so that one may perfectly possess the above three pieces in this world—without harm to others and with a good conscience—and indeed in such a way that one loses nothing of the **Eternal** and the **Future**, but rather gains?

**Answer:** Whoever wishes to attain this more than royal **bliss** <note>original: "Glückseligkeit"; a state of profound happiness or beatitude.</note> must also have more than royal reflections and be of a more than royal mind; he must [seek] the [Wisdom] that always hovers around the **fiery throne of God** <note>A mystical reference to the "Merkabah" or the dwelling place of Divine Majesty.</note> and dwells both in **eternal nature** and in all **creatures**...

§. 10. Therefore, all their labor and expense are in vain. They seek an easy art and difficult labor, when in fact it is a difficult art and easy labor. They spend a great deal of money on materials that can be had for free and cannot be bought in any shop or apothecary, but must be taken directly from nature. Therefore, I call upon all lovers of this art and lead them into the school of wisdom and nature, where the <term>macrocosmic Vulcan</term> <note>The "macrocosmic Vulcan" refers to the universal, creative fire of nature, personified as the Roman god of the forge</note> plays with all animals, vegetables, and minerals as if they were his children, bringing them to perfection—and indeed, through the guidance of an artist who follows nature, bringing them to a state of super-perfection.

§. 14. She, Wisdom alone, is the one who can give complete contentment of the mind, health, and wealth. §. 11. §. 12. Wisdom is such a being that an acquaintance and conversation with her makes all other things sought by the children of men in this world appear contemptible and small in comparison. ->*The Essential Parts of Man.* <note>original Latin: "Partes Hominis eſſentiales."</note><-

How can a human being, consisting of only two essential parts <note>The author is likely referring to the traditional "body and soul" dualism, which he finds insufficient to represent a three-part God.</note>, be an image and imprint of the essential triune God? Furthermore, if all three Persons of the Godhead cooperated only in the creation of man, then it is not unreasonable to ask: which Person in particular made the other things, such as the heavens, the earth, and the elements, along with the rest of the creatures? Does the word **Elohim** <term>Elohim</term> <note>The Hebrew word for God used in Genesis. While it refers to a single God, the ending "-im" is grammatically plural, a fact often used by theologians to argue for the Trinity.</note> not appear in the plural during the creation of all other things, just as it does in the creation of man?

§. 23. But that God is a triune Being is proven by all His triune creatures spoken forth from Him, which—in their natural dissolution and separation—provide us with three essential parts: a **Mercury** <term>Mercurium</term>, a **Sulfur** <term>Sulphur</term>, and a **Salt** <term>Sal</term> <note>Known as the *Tria Prima* (Three Primes) in alchemy. These were not just chemical substances to the author, but universal principles representing the spirit (Mercury), the soul (Sulfur), and the physical body (Salt).</note>, or a spirit, a soul, and a body. And if this revelation of God had not occurred within nature and the creature, truly testifying to the **Trinity** <note>original: "Dreyeinigkeit."</note> of God, how could one have known whether God was only a single, a double, or a triple being? For outside of nature and the creature, God is only One (Deut. 6:4; 4:35; 1 Cor. 8:4), to whom no "Person" can be attributed and who has no name (Exod. 3:14); therefore, He is only a spirit (John 4:24).

§. 29. If you still wish to doubt the substantiality <note>original: "Wesenheit"</note> of God’s Word, then consider that your own words are not without reality. §. 36. Now, when one learns to know this **Something**, one will come closer to Wisdom; for this "Something" is a considerable degree closer to Wisdom than the coarse offspring <note>The "coarse offspring" refers to physical matter in its densest, most corrupted state, furthest from the divine source.</note>, which is nothing else but a dark **matrix** <note>original: *Matrix*; used here to mean a womb or a formative medium in nature</note> that conceives the "Something" (which is filled with the light of Wisdom) as a **First Matter** <term>First Matter: Known in Latin as *materia prima*, this is the primordial, formless substance that exists before it is given a specific physical shape.</term>, and through the **Fiat** <note>original: *Fiat*; the divine command "let there be" that brings the world into being.</note> makes it tangible, so that the **Philosopher** <note>In alchemical texts, "the philosopher" refers to the practitioner of the "Hermetic Art" or alchemy.</note> can handle it, and through the great light of Wisdom can exalt and **tincture** <term>Tincture: To "tinge" or permeate a substance with a new quality, specifically referring to the alchemical process of transforming base metals or the human soul into a higher state.</term> it.

§. 37. But you must take care that you do not spoil the multiplying and attracting power of the conceived "little light" in the fire, for otherwise you would not achieve your purpose. It happens very easily that the most subtle part flies away in the fire; then one retains nothing but a dead **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, which is of no use for any tincture. Natural fire is the most suitable for this, if the right degree of it is achieved, for everything in the **operation** <note>The "operation" is the laboratory process of the alchemical work.</note> depends on that; where this is not observed, and the fire is either too strong, then the **philosophical liquid** <note>original: *philosophische Liquor*</note> will dry out too quickly, the substance will become compact before its time, and nature will not be able to fulfill its proper effect in the impregnation of the work: or, if it is too weak to separate the wateriness from the earth, then the work must "drown," and you will never get to see the **blackness**, and consequently neither the **whiteness** nor the **redness** <note>The three classic stages of the alchemical "Great Work": *nigredo* (blackening/decomposition), *albedo* (whitening/purification), and *rubedo* (reddening/perfection).</note>. §. 38. What some **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophen*; here referring to alchemical authorities.</note> have reported regarding the **preliminary work** <note>original: *Vorarbeit*; the initial preparation of raw materials.</note> in this Great Work has no other basis than this: that **Nature** has already prepared and completed everything belonging to the entire Work—both above and below the earth—without any human intervention. The artist should only take it exactly as Nature has made it. Because the weight of the coarse **darkness** within the earth far exceeds the weight of the concentrated **light** in the mixture, the artist must separate the abundance of darkness from the light very gently and naturally in the "natural furnace," by means of the **mercury of the philosophers** <term>Mercury of the Philosophers: In Latin, *Mercurii Philosophorum*. This is not common quicksilver, but a specialized alchemical substance or "spirit" used to dissolve and purify matter.</term>. But whoever thinks he can perform this preliminary work himself through artifice will find nothing in the end but a constantly new, yet futile, beginning.

**§. 42.** The metals consist of exactly the same essence as the precious stones; the only difference between them is that in precious stones, **Mercury** <note>In alchemical theory, Mercury represents the principle of volatility, fluidity, and spiritual light.</note> predominates among the three principles, whereas in metals, **Sulfur** <note>Sulfur represents the principle of inflammability, soul, and heat.</note> predominates. For Mercury is fundamentally a pure light, and Sulfur is fundamentally a pure fire. Fire is thick, and light is thin; for this reason, precious stones are transparent and metals are not. The less Sulfur there is in the precious stones, the more luminous and clear they are; and the more Sulfur there is in the metals, the denser and more fixed <note>A "fixed" substance in alchemy is one that can withstand the heat of fire without evaporating.</note> they are. **Salt**, however—because it is born of Sulfur and Mercury—is their corporeality and magnet, in which the first two (Sulfur and Mercury) take hold of one another and become tangible. Depending on whether the **Matrix** <term>original: *Matrix*; the "womb" or gestational environment in the earth where minerals grow.</term> or their salt was pure or impure, pure or impure metals or precious stones were formed.

**§. 42.** Metals consist of the very same essence as precious stones; the only difference between them is that in precious stones—according to the three principles—**Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*; in alchemical theory, the principle of fluidity, volatility, and the life-spirit.</note> predominates, while in metals, **Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur*; the alchemical principle of combustibility, heat, and the soul.</note> predominates. **§. 46.** I must also repeat how from a fiery darkness and a watery light—as two **opposites** <note>original: *Contrariis*</note> and spiritual, eternal **principles** <note>original: *Principiis*</note>—all temporal, visible, and tangible things were created according to the distinction of the three kingdoms of nature <note>The "three kingdoms" refers to the traditional classification of the natural world into Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal.</note>. **§. 47.** That these are not my own ideas, but the true **principles** of God’s creation, I will clearly prove both from the words of **Moses** <note>original: *Mosis*; a reference to the account of creation in the Book of Genesis.</note> and from nature itself. For when **Moses** says of each day’s work of God that from evening and morning the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, and sixth days came to be, he intends to indicate nothing else by "evening" and "morning" than these two **originating principles** <note>original: *Principia Principiantia*</note>: darkness and light. He shows that from these two—as they inclined from spirituality toward physicality and became **chaotic** <note>original: *chaotisch*; in this context, it refers to the "Chaos" or the *Prima Materia*, the unformed primal substance from which God organized the universe.</note>—all the essential days of creation emerged through the divine **command** <note>original: *Fiat*; referencing the Latin "Fiat Lux" (Let there be light).</note>. **§. 48.** Darkness has existed in God from all eternity just as much as the light. <meta>This page continues the metaphysical exploration of the relationship between light and darkness. ...darkness? <note>original: *Finsterniß?*</note> Isaiah 45, verse 7 <note>"I form the light, and create darkness: I make peace, and create evil: I the Lord do all these things."</note>. Must not the darkness carry the light? As can be seen in every lit candle, which could not shine without a dark wick.

### §. 49.
As deep as the light is now hidden here below in the darkness, just as deep—and even deeper—was the darkness hidden within the light before the Creation. The light first gave birth to the darkness, and now the darkness must in turn give birth to the light; this is the greatest wonder of **Wisdom** <note>The author likely refers to *Sophia*, the personified Divine Wisdom, a central figure in Christian theosophy who mediates between God and the world.</note>.

### §. 50.
The darkness is separated from the light as the woman is from the man, Genesis 2:21, 22, 23 <note>The account of Eve being formed from Adam’s side.</note>. And just as the woman retained much from the male **essence** <term>essence: original German *Essenz*, referring to the fundamental, spiritual substance of a being.</term> during the separation, so also the darkness retained much from the light-essence during the separation, for they cannot be entirely separated. For this reason, the darkness now longs for the light, because they both originally belong together as one, and lived together in the highest harmony; and the light longs for the darkness, so that it might reveal itself and be felt within it. Now, when these two are brought into such a **balance** <term>balance: original German *Temperanz*, an alchemical and medical term for the harmonious mixing of opposing qualities (like hot and cold, or light and dark).</term> in external nature that the light covers the earth, it becomes a **substance** <term>substance: original German *Wesen*, meaning an entity, being, or manifested matter.</term> that can temper all adversities, both in human bodies and in all other bodies created from light and darkness.

<meta>catchword: §. 51.</meta>

<summary>This page explains the reciprocal relationship between light and darkness, comparing their separation and mutual longing to the biblical creation of man and woman. It suggests that a perfect balance of these forces can heal or "temper" the physical world.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical and theological instructions for the "new creation," transitioning from the general relationship of light and darkness to the specific practical steps of the "Great Work" within the human microcosm.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>

### §. 51.
However, in this microcosmic new creation <note>The "microcosm" refers to the human being as a miniature reflection of the entire universe.</note>, you must be primarily intent upon first turning all your elements—both the lower and the upper—into a **philosophical water**, in which light and darkness, life and death, are present together, just as in Genesis 1:2 <note>"And the earth was without form, and void; and darkness was upon the face of the deep. And the Spirit of God moved upon the face of the waters."</note>. When this has been accomplished, you must very gently separate the water from the earth in the divine fire, so that the earth hidden within such water may gradually emerge. And when all the water is separated from the earth, and your soil is covered with darkness, the **heavenly seed** is sown into it. This seed afterward proves its flowering through many and various colors, and then enters into a white, and finally into a red immortal fruit, for which the Creator is to be highly praised. 

This darkness is the desired **philosophical blackness** <term>philosophical blackness: also known as *nigredo*, the first stage of the alchemical process involving putrefaction and the stripping away of old forms.</term>, which carries a delicate light hidden within itself; it is a virgin and completely empty earth that has never yet bore or given birth, which desires the light from its very heart, and upon which the light and the whole of heaven, in turn, have their eyes fixed. It is the true **magnet** <note>In alchemical thought, the "magnet" is a substance or state that has the power to attract celestial influences or the "astral spirit" down into the material realm.</note> that draws the light of the heavenly sun and moon to itself, and retains and **fixes** <term>fixes: original German *figiret*, an alchemical term for making a volatile substance stable and solid.</term> it. If the great soil of the world were not thoroughly provided by God with such a magnet—which draws down the powers of heaven and communicates them to us through the creatures for our nourishment—how would it stand with our natural life? The astral-

But it is the **astral fire** <term>astral fire: a celestial or cosmic energy believed by alchemists to flow from the stars to influence life on Earth</term> that constantly maintains the **central fire** <note>The "central fire" refers to the vital heat at the core of the Earth or within the heart of a living being.</note> and promotes the growth of earth's plants; through this, the natural life of humans and animals—which consists of fire and light—is sustained.

§. 52. If we could enjoy this fire and light purely and without the mixture of coarse, dark essences, we would become immortal in our natural life, *Genesis 3:22* <note>"And now, lest he put forth his hand, and take also of the tree of life, and eat, and live for ever."</note>. Therefore, growth comes solely from the light mixed with fire, and not from the dark mass <note>original: *Klumpen*</note>, which is only a covering for the light and a **matrix** <term>matrix: a womb or foundational substance in which something is shaped or takes root</term> in which the subtle light takes hold and becomes visible.

§. 53. Just as in the earth the fire and light are on the inside and the darkness is on the outside, it is exactly the reverse with the sun, which is dark in its interior and luminous on its exterior; but because the quantity of its light exceeds the quantity of darkness by so many times, the darkness can no more rise up within it than in God Himself. Instead, the darkness remains **passive** <note>original: *patiens*</note> and the light remains **active** <note>original: *agens*</note>; therefore, the sun is the most beautiful mirror of divine wisdom and glory. Its outgoing rays of fire and light are pure **spiritual tinctures** <term>tinctures: in alchemy, these are potent, spiritualized essences capable of healing, transforming, or "tinging" base matter into a higher state</term>, through which all lower plants and creatures—which in winter are as if dead, putrefied, and through this **putrefaction** <note>original: *Putrefaction*; the alchemical stage of decay (nigredo) necessary for new life to emerge.</note> are to some extent released <note>original: *solvirte*, meaning dissolved or loosened from a bound state</note> from the shackles of the curse—are once again **enlivened** <note>The original German breaks the word *be-lebt* (enlivened/revived) across the page break.</note> in the spring.

Of these four, two are always set against two; otherwise, there would be no life and no movement. For a simple being <note>A being composed of only one unmixed element or quality.</note> cannot move itself, because it is moved by nothing contrary to it; but the contrary quality in all things creates a pleasant enmity <note>The author suggests that growth and vitality require a dynamic tension or "strife" between opposing forces, such as hot and cold or wet and dry.</note> in all of nature. Therefore, one element drives the other, and in such driving, they promote the births of animals, vegetables, and minerals.

§. 55. Fire and water are entirely opposed to one another, and they require the air for their union. The air was composed by God out of a more subtle water and a coarser fire, so that water might join and unite with water, and fire with fire, through magnetic attraction. Air and earth are likewise diametrically opposed <note>original: *e diametro*</note> to one another. However, so that both might come together for the birthing of all things and contribute their part, Wisdom—acting as the master-builder of creation—set water, consisting of a subtle earth and a coarse air, as their **mediator** <note>original: *Mediatore*; a go-between that allows two incompatible things to connect.</note>. For we see daily how the central fire <term>central fire: in early modern science, a heat source believed to exist at the center of the Earth that drives geological and biological processes.</term> gradually <note>original: *gradatim*</note> drives the pure and subtle atoms of the earth up into the air by means of water, and further toward the sphere of fire for impregnation. Once these atoms have conceived from the fire and air, they descend again by degrees <note>original: *per gradus*</note> and bring it to the Earth, the universal mother, for its final birth, as referenced in Hosea 2:21, 22 <note>This biblical passage describes a chain of creation where the Lord answers the heavens, and the heavens answer the earth.</note>. This occurs frequently, partly through rain and partly through dew, until the earth has enough of the upper masculine seed for the birthing of her children. This is God’s unceasing comedy of love and the delight of the philosophers <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers to alchemists or "natural philosophers" who studied the secrets of nature.</note>.

§. 56. However, that which rises from the earth into the air is not pure water, but is mixed with a subtle earth; this can be perceived when one [collects] rainwater...

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the alchemical artist, moving from the allegorical "king bee" to the specific manipulation of the elements using the "philosophical water." It explains how the laboratory work must mirror the natural cycles of the universe.</meta>

-> <image-desc>A horizontal decorative woodcut ornament featuring a central flower flanked by symmetrical scrolled foliage and vine patterns.</image-desc> <-

...be found in the ground, he must begin with the <term>philosophical water: a symbolic universal solvent or "mercury" believed to contain the essence of all four elements</term> <note>original: *philosophischen Wasser*</note>, in which all elements are together. For with this, he must make the coarse subtle and the subtle tangible. That is, he must <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solviren*</note> the fire and light compacted within the earth—and especially within the metallic workshops <note>likely referring to the natural veins or ores where metals "grow" in the earth</note>—and with this <term>solution</term> <note>original: *Solution*</note>, magnetically draw down and <term>fix</term> <note>original: *figiren*; to make a volatile spirit or gas into a stable, solid substance</note> the upper fire and light.

§. 61. What the two lower elements—earth and water—have thickened at one time, the two upper elements—fire and air—thin or refine <note>original: *subtilisiren*</note> at another time by means of the water. Indeed, one element is always <term>transmuting</term> <note>original: *transmutiret*</note> into the other, which nonetheless cannot happen without the mediation of the other elements. For by **descending** <note>original Latin: *descendendo*</note>, the upper fire becomes air in the air, the air becomes water in the water, the water becomes earth in the earth, and the earth becomes fire in the fire. In **ascending** <note>original Latin: *ascendendo*</note>, however, the earth becomes water in the water, the water becomes air in the air, the air becomes fire in the fire, and the fire becomes earth in the earth. In short: just as the descent occurs by **steps** <note>original Latin: *per Gradus*</note>, the ascent also occurs through those very same steps. This **gradation** <note>original: *Gradation*</note> you must practice on a small scale <note>referring to the laboratory or "microcosm"</note> just as nature does on a large scale <note>the universe or "macrocosm"</note>, yet in such a way that the small is entirely and completely governed by the large.

<meta>This page transitions from the technical description of element transmutation discussed on the previous page to the spiritual and cosmological origin of those elements. It identifies a single "prime" element—Fire and Light—which underlies the four physical elements and connects the alchemical work to the biblical narrative of Eden.</meta>

-> <image-desc>An ornate woodcut headpiece consisting of symmetrical leafy scrolls flanking a central stylized flower or jewel.</image-desc> <-

§. 62. Although various things have been written concerning four elements in this report and in other philosophies, you should nonetheless know that, fundamentally, there is no more than a single element consisting of fire and light. This is the eternal nature, which immediately precedes the external nature in the act of creation, and which still dwells invisibly through the whole of external nature and the four elements, preserving them and making them fruitful. This element is the universal seed for the maintenance of the entire creation; it is *particularized* <note>original: *particularisiret*; meaning the universal essence is funneled into specific, individual forms</note> in every creature through the divine **Fiat** <note>Latin: "Let it be done"; the creative command of God</note>, but in Man it remains *universal*, as he is a *microcosm* <note>original: *microcosmo*; a "little world" that mirrors the entire universe</note> or *compendium* <note>original: *compendio*; a condensed summary or collection</note> of the whole world.

§. 63. Saint **Moses** calls this single element a river flowing out of **Eden** to water the garden of **Paradise**, which was planted by the Father of Light without human hands. Man, who was created from this single element according to the likeness of **God**, was intended to cultivate and protect this garden. There, he was to increase his lineage in a paradisiacal manner and, in time, *extend* <note>original: *extendiren*</note> that lineage, along with **Paradise**, through the whole world, according to **Genesis 1:26**. For **Paradise** was only manifest in one place—namely, in **Eden**—while the rest of the earth, along with its vegetation, had been corrupted by **Lucifer**. Man, in his innate form of light <note>original: *Lichtsgestalt*</note>, was meant to *successively* <note>gradually; step by step</note> make the earth entirely subject to himself.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 41 regarding the spiritual role of Adam. -> <image-desc>A horizontal decorative typographic ornament consisting of symmetrical scrollwork, foliage, and a central floral element.</image-desc> <-

it is written: Adam was meant to preserve the Paradise planted by **God** and continue to cultivate it until the entire surface of the earth were nothing but Paradise; this might very well have come to pass, if only he had not eaten of the mortal fruit in the middle of the garden—where Good and Evil or Light and Darkness stood in equal **balance** <note>original: *Bilance*</note>—but had instead eaten only of the immortal fruit toward the **East** <note>original: *gegen den Morgen*; literally "toward the morning," a traditional reference to the East as the source of divine light</note>, where the darkness was swallowed up by the light. Thereby he would have propagated and spread his **lineage** <note>original: *Geschlecht*; referring to the human race</note> and Paradise according to the immortal way. And just as man now increases his lineage in the mortal quality, and has gradually cultivated the whole earth with mortal fruits and made it subject to himself, so could that other [way] have happened just as well—even more easily—and indeed without toil and sorrow. In this, man was meant to be God’s assistant and a mediator, to set right again the **hierarchy** <note>original: *Hierarchie*; in this context, it refers to the celestial order or the "thrones" of the angels that were left vacant or corrupted by the fall of Lucifer</note> corrupted by **Lucifer**.

§. 64. But when **Lucifer** saw that his dark power was to be restricted and his ignited wrath swallowed up again by love, and that he could not overcome with force the man who was protected everywhere by light, he paid heed to all his (the man’s) actions and expressions, and lay in wait for him in the **middle realm of nature** <note>original: *Mittelreich der Natur*; a cosmological zone where spiritual light and material darkness overlap and compete</note>, where light and darkness, good and evil, death and life [were] so mixed together with one another—

...fiery, so that one could not be distinguished from the other.

§. 65. Lucifer had an entrance into this realm by way of the **principle** <term>principle <note>original: *principium*; in this mystical context, it refers to one of the fundamental source-energies of reality—darkness, light, or the material world</note></term> of darkness. He thought: "If man ever wanders out from his fortress into this **middle realm** <note>the intermediate spiritual space between the heavenly and hellish realms</note>, you will have the perfect opportunity to bring about his fall." And because the tree in the middle of the garden was beautiful to behold, and its fruits seemed pleasant to eat, and man became infatuated with it, Lucifer took advantage of this situation.

§. 66. So that he would not be recognized in his dark ferocity, he sent a well-adorned and eloquent emissary there from his kingdom; this was the **serpent**. It positioned itself by the **Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil**, which had grown out of the realm of mortality. Following its instructions, it spoke kindly to man; among other persuasions, it specifically used the argument that the tree would bring wisdom and make men equal to **God**.

§. 74. Miners are well aware of this, for in the depths of the earth, their breath is often cut off by the coarseness of the air, and their lamps are extinguished by it.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 49 regarding the alchemical refinement of metals. The previous page argued that while metals contain a "universal seed," they are trapped in the "coarse air" of the earth and cannot multiply. Page 50 explains how the alchemist can release this seed and multiply its power tenfold through the "Mercury of the Philosophers."</meta>

<page-num>50</page-num>

...minerals up into the **vegetable** kingdom, freed the seed of **metals** from its **compaction** <note>original: *Compaction*; the state of being densely packed or solidified into a physical metal.</note> through its **homogeneous Mercury**, and through the upper **elements**, multiplied it, elevated it, and brought it to a **tincture**.

§. 76. Although the metallic seed is in and of itself already a **medicine** and **tincture**, it is nevertheless only simple, and before its multiplication, it cannot impart anything to others, because it possesses no more than it needs for itself. But if it is placed into its **matrix** <note>original: *Matricem*; the "womb" or environment required for alchemical growth.</note>, that is, into the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Mercurium Philosophorum*; not common quicksilver, but a refined, "living" substance alchemists believed could dissolve and nourish the metallic seed.</note> from which it was initially born, then according to the weight of nature, as much metallic seed enters the **matrix** of the tenth part as the **matrix** itself comprises. 

This light-fiery seed then begins to struggle within the watery **matrix**. Because it receives sufficient aid from both the **astral** <note>original: *astralischen*; refers to the celestial or "star-born" spiritual influences.</note> and **central** fire <note>original: *centralischen*; the internal heat or life-force at the center of matter.</note>, while the access of the wateriness (as a coarser element) is barred, it gradually overcomes the **Mercury** as its mother. It swallows her and **transmutes** her entirely into itself. Through this, it becomes ten times as strong and powerful as it was before, and through this first **multiplication** <note>original: *Multiplication*; the specific alchemical process of increasing the quantity and potency of the Tincture or Philosopher's Stone.</note>, it can help and impart its power to ten others.

§. 77. But regarding where **metals** originally came from, and still come from, I wish to show briefly and fundamentally through the light granted to me by God and Nature. When, through the fall of **Lucifer**, his fire was turned into earth and his light into water, thus prompting God to a new creation—namely, the creation of this world—the pure fire and light did not entirely depart from these fallen elements, earth and water; rather, it was contracted together with the earth and water through the **Fiat** <note>original: *Fiat*; the divine command "Let there be," used in Genesis to initiate creation.</note>. The subtle part of it, at God’s command, moved into the heights, from which the **firmament** and the **stars** were formed; the thick and coarse part, however, according to the will of the Creator, settled into the center of this **globe**, which the philosophers call the **central fire**.

§. 78. §. 80. From this **Mercury** all metals and minerals are born, for it is their seed, which consists of fire and light: according to the nature and condition of its workshop in the earth, it builds for itself a **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> or a house from its own essence, in which it dwells. Such a body is either gold, silver, copper, or lead, etc., all according to the condition of the **Matrix** <note>original: *Matrix*; here meaning the geological "womb" or environment where the metal forms.</note> or the workshop, and depending on whether it has worked for a long or short time on such a house or body. §. 81. Along this path, many millions of people have worked themselves into poverty, sickness, and death, and have found nothing; it seems to me just as if someone chased an inhabitant out of a house, and afterwards wanted to turn the house itself into another inhabitant. How well would he be following Nature then, and how well would he succeed? Would not all people consider him witless? It is exactly the same with those who [take] a mine[ral]—

§. 83. I have shown you here a foundation upon which all divine and natural **wisdom** rests; if you now build upon this and apply it correctly, you have found what you seek. For through a thorough knowledge of the **creatures** <note>In this 17th-18th century context, "creatures" refers to all created things in the natural world, from minerals to animals.</note>, you have become acquainted with your **Creator**, which acquaintance places your soul in complete satisfaction and peace. Beyond this, He gives you a jewel, namely **Urim and Thummim** <note>original: *Urim und Thumin*. In the Hebrew Bible, these were objects used by the High Priest to divine the will of God. Here, the author uses them to symbolize a direct spiritual connection to divine truth.</note>, or **Fire and Light**, through which you may ask the Lord in all your undertakings and in all apparent danger, and receive a certain answer; it also perfectly preserves your body from disease and poverty. And when the end of your life approaches, you can know this for certain beforehand when the **Tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*. In alchemy, the "Tincture" is the refined essence of the Philosopher’s Stone, believed to be a universal medicine that can cure any illness and prolong life until one's predestined time.</note> no longer wishes to work within you. And because you have become so well-acquainted with God—as one friend with another—you will not dread **death** at all, which then is a gentle falling asleep without any sickness or woe; rather, you are joyful and comforted to now enter fully into the **land of the living**, which you have seen here only from afar.

[...and comfort] and of **darkness**, likewise of the **blessing** and **curse** arising from it—if my meaning is understood correctly—I say once again: that darkness, as long as it is passive <note>original: *leidend*. In this context, it means "passive" or "acted upon," as opposed to "active."</note> and allows itself to be ruled by the **light**, may not be called a curse; rather, it is a curse only where it is active <note>original: *würkend*. §. 85. The basis and cause of such transmutation is the **fixed fire** <note>original: *fixe Feuer*. In alchemy, "fixed" refers to a substance that is stable and resistant to the heat of fire, unlike "volatile" substances that evaporate.</note> locked into the **Center** <note>original: *Centrum*</note> of the earth by the **Creator** at the beginning, and the **fixed light** extended from such fire throughout the whole earth, which has been preserved in its degree until now, and still lies hidden and imprisoned in the coarse darkness as a **seed** for the future new earth. When this same seed, one day by the ordaining of the Creator, shall receive sufficient help and **succor** <note>original: *Succurs*. Meaning aid, help, or reinforcements.</note> from the upper fire and light, it will be mightily strengthened in its degree and greatly ignited. In such an ignition, it will drive all waters, and with the waters all **volatile atoms** <note>original: *flüchtige Atomen*. In early modern science, "atoms" referred to the smallest constituent particles of matter; "volatile" refers to those that easily turn into gas or vapor.</note> of the earth, from all sides into the air.

When the waters, which until now have prevented the full breakout of such a **central fire** <note>original: *centralischen Feuers*. In alchemical thought, the "central fire" is the hidden, life-giving heat at the heart of the earth or any substance.</note>, shall now be taken away, then this fire will break forth from its prison on all sides, overcome the coarse darkness and the curse of the earth as its enemy, and successively burn it entirely, so that nothing will remain but a pure, **fixed mass** <note>In alchemy, a "fixed" substance is one that is stable and can withstand the heat of fire without evaporating.</note>.

§. 86. §. 89. Regarding the phenomena just mentioned, no other **physical reason** <note>original Latin: *Ratio Physica*.</note> can be reasonably given than that they are outbreaks of the **central fire** <note>In the author's worldview, the "central fire" is a hidden source of heat at the Earth's core, which alchemists believed drove both geological change and spiritual refinement.</note>. This is also to be concluded because their rays always rise up from the Earth and persist in the upper air like a great, constant fire.

§. 90. In the northern countries, such phenomena have been known for quite some time, but they have never been as large or as frequent as they are now. Just as any fire, when it gains power, spreads further and further around itself, so too does the central fire; according to the evidence of these phenomena, within a few years it has extended itself very far toward the **South** <note>original: *Mittag* (Midday).</note> and toward the **West** <note>original: *Abend* (Evening).</note> from the **North** <note>original: *Mitternacht* (Midnight). These terms are traditional German ways of naming cardinal directions based on the position of the sun.</note>.

§. 91. One will find in no **chronicle** <note>A historical record or ledger of significant events.</note> that outbreaks of this kind were ever seen in our regions until the year 1716. On the 17th of March, the first one occurred here; and because it was something entirely unusual at that time and broke out quite unexpectedly, it caused great **consternation** and terror among both the children of God and the children of this world. However, the former—who were already somewhat accustomed to both divine and natural light—soon took heart, praised God in His glory, and rejoiced greatly at the brilliance of this majestic light. The others, however,

§. 92. But as the loving and long-suffering God takes no pleasure in the death and destruction of the sinner, but rather desires that he change his mind and spirit, He thus warns the complacent world through such phenomena and real threats, calling it back from its ruin. But in the wide world, people no longer want to hear God's voice or see His wonders. It is now as it was in the days of **Noah** <note>Refers to the biblical story of the Great Flood, where people ignored warnings of coming judgment.</note>, etc. Because God did not strike immediately the first time and allow the end to come, people now consider these divine heralds to be something entirely natural; they thus separate God and Nature, the Creator and the creatures, far from one another. Yet one should rightly consider that God and Nature are as closely united—indeed, even more closely—as body and soul, and that God works in no other way than in Nature and through Nature.

<meta>This page continues the author's personal observations and philosophical reflections on the celestial phenomena (the Aurora Borealis) mentioned on the previous page. Having described the public’s fear, the author now provides a first-hand account of the light's intensity and its place within his mystical-scientific worldview.</meta>

<page-num>61</page-num>
<sig>F 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: §. 95.</meta>

-><image-desc>A decorative printer's ornament featuring symmetrical floral and scroll motifs is centered at the top of the page.</image-desc><-

§. 93. Among all the **phenomena** <note>The author uses this term to refer to the Aurora Borealis, or Northern Lights, which were poorly understood at the time.</note> that I have seen, none has appeared greater or more powerful to me than the first; for at the place where I was at that time, it was so bright that I could not only read all manner of writing, but also, along with others, quite clearly hear the rising rays as a **gentle hissing or rushing sound** <note>This "sound" of the aurora is a subject of historical debate; while many observers in the 18th century reported it, modern scientists have only recently begun to explain how electromagnetic effects might produce audible noise.</note>, and it seemed as if I were in the middle, or in the **center** of this appearing light. Although others have told me that they observed this same thing afterward during other phenomena, it all depended on whether the outbreaks of the **central fire** <term>central fire: In the author's worldview, a hidden energy or spiritual fire within the Earth that occasionally bursts forth.</term> were large or small in a given place. From this light breaking forth out of the darkness, one can to some extent recognize how a light could appear through God’s speaking before the creation of the sun, moon, and stars, as seen in **Genesis 1:3** <note>original: "Gen. I, 3." The biblical verse where God says, "Let there be light."</note>.

§. 94. This **central fire** is of a cold quality, and the **astral fire** <term>astral fire: Celestial or "starry" energy that descends from the heavens, often contrasted with the internal fire of the Earth.</term> is of a warm quality. Between these two fires, the greatest mysteries occur, and all **vegetation** <note>original: "Vegetabilien."</note> is brought to life <note>literally "boiled out" or distilled; a term borrowed from alchemy to describe the process of growth.</note> through them. Both serve the creation of God and the preservation and increase of all **creatures**. The Earth, however, is the **mother**, who through her powerful **Fiat** <note>Latin for "let it be"; the word used by God to bring the world into existence.</note> makes both forces physical and brings them forth according to the **macrocosmic idea** <term>macrocosmic idea: The divine blueprint or organizational pattern of the entire universe.</term>. What one of these two creates, the other breaks apart, and always makes something new from it again, as a demonstration of the great wonders of God.

<summary>The author describes hearing a hissing sound during an exceptionally bright aurora and uses the event to explain how biblical light existed before the sun. He presents a cosmological theory where life is generated by the interaction of "cold" internal fire and "warm" celestial fire.</summary>

<keywords>Aurora Borealis, central fire, astral fire, Genesis, creation, macrocosm, alchemy, nature philosophy</keywords>
