<meta>This page continues the author’s exploration of the metaphysical origins of matter. Having previously discussed the Aurora Borealis as an outbreak of "central fire," the author now explains the mechanics of how spiritual energy becomes physical substance through the tension of opposing forces.</meta>

<page-num>62</page-num>
<header><image-desc>horizontal ornamental flourish</image-desc></header>

§. 95. If these two opposing qualities did not exist, there would be no **corporeality** <note>original: "Leiblichkeit"; the state of having a physical body or material existence.</note>; for through their conflict, the spirits become exhausted and **precipitate** <note>In an alchemical sense, to "precipitate" means to fall out of a spiritual or vaporous state and become solid matter.</note> themselves, becoming corporeal. The warm fire is above, and the cold is below. Although the cold is also within the warm, and the warm within the cold, it is simply that the warm predominates above, and the cold predominates below. Likewise, the warm is in the light, and the cold is in the darkness. If you now understand how the spirits become corporeal through these two fires, you will also learn to see how bodies can become spiritual again, and through such <term>Science</term> <note>original: "Scienz"; the author uses this to mean a higher, spiritual knowledge or "sacred art" rather than modern materialist science.</note> you will find success in your philosophy; for in this science, everything depends upon the "corporeal-making" of the macrocosmic spirits.

§. 96. Just as the rays of the warm fire are **congealed** <note>original: "congeliret"; to freeze or solidify.</note> by the cold, and the rays of the cold fire are **coagulated** <note>original: "coaguliret"; to thicken or change from a fluid to a solid state.</note> by the warm: so, in turn, the warm fire dissolves the congealing of the cold, and the cold dissolves the coagulation of the warm fire. Basilius Valentinus <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist and monk; his writings were highly influential in the development of early chemistry and Hermetic philosophy.</note> philosophizes quite magnificently on this throughout his entire *Mining Book* <note>original: "Bergbuche"; likely referring to one of the metallurgical or mineralogical treatises attributed to Valentinus.</note>, especially in the 48th and 49th chapters. With and through these two primary qualities, divine wisdom works and creates all things; and in imitation of this, the philosophers also make their stone or <term>Tincture</term> <note>Tincture: Often synonymous with the "Philosopher's Stone," a substance believed to be capable of transforming base metals into gold and perfecting the human soul.</note>.

<meta>catchword: §. 97.</meta>

<summary>This page explains the alchemical process of corporeality, arguing that physical matter is created by the tension and "precipitation" of opposing warm and cold spiritual fires. It positions this understanding as the key to the "Science" of transforming bodies back into spirits, citing the authority of the alchemist Basilius Valentinus.</summary>
<keywords>corporeality, spirits, alchemy, warm fire, cold fire, Basilius Valentinus, precipitation, coagulation, congealing, Philosopher's Stone, Tincture</keywords>

§. 97. For this reason, these same bodies—because they are taken from the cold essence of the earth (Genesis 2:7) and are **coagulated** <note>original: "coaguliret"; thickened or solidified from a fluid or spiritual state.</note> by the warm fire of our soul through the continuous breathing-in or **enspiriting** <note>original: "Eingeisten"; the act of God infusing a physical form with spirit.</note> of God—are buried again in the earth after the departure of the soul. This is done so that they may be **putrefied** <note>original: "putrificiret"; the process of decay which, in alchemy, is the essential first step toward purification and rebirth.</note> by the **central fire** <note>The author believes a spiritual heat exists within the center of the earth that helps drive natural processes.</note> that constantly emanates up to the surface of the earth. Thus, by **rotting** <note>original Latin: "putrefcendo"; the process of decaying or turning to dust.</note>, they are released from the bonds of mortality and can be inseparably reunited with the departed soul. Meanwhile, the soul does not cease to correspond with its body until it is completely reunited with it.

§. 98. For this very reason, it is against the order of God and Nature when the dead in the earth are disturbed from their rest by digging up their bones while preparing new graves, and either leaving them—if not entirely, then in part—lying in the churchyards, or even throwing them into the **charnel houses** <term>charnel houses</term> <note>original: "Beinhäuser"; buildings or vaults where human skeletal remains were stored when old graves were cleared for new ones.</note> built for that purpose in many places; there they may lie for several hundred years and never reach a state of **decomposition** <note>original: "Verwesung"; the natural breakdown of the body which the author sees as a necessary spiritual release.</note>.

than we. Since people have become all too fond of moralizing and have abandoned **natural science** <note>original: "Physik"; in this context, it refers to the study of the material world and the laws of nature rather than modern physics.</note>, this knowledge has been lost to us along with other natural sciences. The ancients were more sensible in all such natural matters, and, according to the then-flourishing **natural law** <note>original: "Rechte der Natur"; the philosophical idea that certain rights or laws are inherent by virtue of human nature and observable in the world.</note>, every **head of the household** <note>original: "Hausvater"; a term implying the patriarchal leader of a multi-generational home and estate.</note> was permitted to bury his dead either in his field or in his garden, wherever he believed they could lie most securely. 

Nowadays, however, since natural law is no longer practicable, and a self-willed and self-interested law has been put in its place, this freedom is entirely cut off. In every town and every village there is a small plot of land which bears no comparison to the multitude of inhabitants; there, over so many hundreds of years, all the dead have been buried. And if the world were to stand for many thousands more years, according to current regulations, all those dying in such a time would have to be brought to that same place. 

This is just as if someone sowed several **wispels** <note>original: "Wispel"; an old Northern German unit of dry measure for grain, roughly equivalent to 24 to 30 bushels.</note> of grain on a single field, and then, before the same could even begin to grow, brought even more to it every day, and furthermore constantly dug and plowed in that field; it is easy to imagine what kind of fruit one would expect to harvest from it.

...I say, that certainly the vegetable fruits <note>original: "vegetabilischen Früchte"; referring here to all plant-based food and crops harvested from the earth.</note> which man enjoys are of the same essence and being as human bodies, and only *differ in degree* <note>original: "gradu differiren"</note>, according to the *Axiom: We are nourished by the same things from which we are composed* <note>original: "Ex iisdem nutrimur, ex quibus constamus." §. 103. In decomposition <note>original: "Verwesung"; the natural process of decay after death.</note>, the body will suffer neither torment nor loss, for it is innocent and unfeeling, as we can observe in every lifeless body. During its time on earth, the body acted only as a *purely passive subject* <note>original: "pures patiens"; a philosophical term for a being that does not act of its own will but is merely acted upon by another force.</note> which had to do whatever it was driven to by the soul with force, whether good or evil. All sensation, whether it be good or evil, lies in the soul, just as all effects, both good and evil, come from it. It will be a great harm and a pain to that soul when it must see all its imagined goods—such as beauty, lust, honor, wealth, and glory—perish in the divine fire, and see itself entirely stripped and impoverished before the face of God and all the saints; while on the other hand, seeing others adorned and clothed with glory and splendor from the divine light.

§. 104. The degrees of blessedness will be very different, depending entirely on how much or how little a person has renounced here in the world, and conversely, how much or how little of the divine light-powers <note>original: "Lichtskräfte"; in mystical and Pietist thought, these are spiritual energies or "seeds" of light gathered through a holy life to form the "spiritual body" in the afterlife.</note> they have gathered and *attracted* <note>original: "attrahiret"</note> into their soul for the future new body, *1 Corinthians 15* <note>The author refers to the New Testament chapter that discusses the "spiritual body" and how "one star differs from another star in glory."</note>.

The transfiguration of our bodies depends on nothing else than the soul, which in this time is anointed or <term>tinctured</term> <note>original: "tingirten"; an alchemical term referring to the process of imbuing a substance with a specific quality, power, or color—here used to describe the soul being "dyed" with divine light.</note> by Jehovah, or by the Spirit of Christ. Now, if that same soul has gathered much oil—referring to the parable in Matthew 25 <note>The Parable of the Ten Virgins, where oil represents spiritual preparation and the presence of the Holy Spirit.</note>—or divine light and life before its departure from the body, then the new body, in union with such a soul, will possess great radiance. However, if it had little light, it will also impart little radiance to its body there in the resurrection, and a small light in a large lantern will make a poor showing. And for this very reason, people will differ in their radiance, just as the stars in the heavens differ <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 15:41, explaining that the glory of the resurrected bodies will vary in intensity.</note>.

§. 105. Our current natural body is, by way of comparison, like <term>congealed</term> <note>original: "congelirt"; frozen or solidified into a mass.</note> or frozen water: as long as the frost is within it, nothing can penetrate it unless the opening is as large as the piece of ice or the body itself. But when the frost is out and the ice is dissolved by heat, it becomes subtle and can pass through all <term>pores</term> <note>original: "Poros"; tiny channels or openings in a substance.</note>. It is exactly the same with our bodies: once the <term>coagulation</term> <note>original: "Coagulation"; the thickening or hardening of a substance into a solid state.</note> is removed, they become subtle, like spirits, and like Christ after his resurrection—able to pass through closed doors and to <term>penetrate</term> <note>original: "penetriren"</note> all things. And just as the entire piece of ice is <term>dissolved</term> <note>original: "solviret"</note> and <term>transmuted</term> <note>original: "transmutiret"; transformed from one state or substance into another.</note> into water by heat, so too will our entire natural body be dissolved by the cold fire <note>In alchemical thought, "cold fire" often refers to a spiritual or philosophical solvent that transforms matter without the destructive heat of common fire.</note>.

<summary>This page explains that the glory of a resurrected body is determined by the spiritual "oil" or light the soul gathered during life. It uses the analogy of ice melting into water to describe how the dense physical body becomes a "subtle" body capable of passing through solid matter.</summary>

and made spiritual, so that the entire human machine <note>original: "menschliche Maschine"; a common 17th and 18th-century metaphor describing the biological body as a complex mechanical system designed by God.</note>, according to the greater or lesser degree of light (1 Cor. 15:40–41), enters into a new body. §. 106. Just as this <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "Putrefaction"; the process of decay or rotting, viewed here as a necessary stage of purification.</note> and <term>solution</term> <note>original: "Solution"; the alchemical process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</note> does not happen <note>original: "uno momento"</note> in a single moment or all at once (as described in 1 Cor. 15:52), but rather occurs gradually and indeed <note>original: "actu continuo"</note> as a continuous process because the central fire <note>original: "centralischen Feuers"; in alchemical philosophy, a vital heat or "inner sun" that drives growth and transformation from within the earth or the body.</note> does not yet have full power; so too does the resurrection and the reunion of the soul and the body happen as a continuous process (1 Thess. 4:15). Then, at last, after the entire human body has lain in decay for its appointed time, nothing more remains of it than a tiny amount of earth—enough to hold in one hand—into which eternal darkness had found an entrance, as if into its own physical child; this darkness is also the cause of all illnesses, indeed, of death itself.

§. 107. Adam already possessed something of such earth within and upon himself in Paradise as a *potential* <note>original: "Potentiam"; a Latin term referring here to an inherent capacity or latent power.</note> for mortality; otherwise, he could not have lusted after the mortal fruit, nor could he have become mortal. For this reason, God also had to breathe a twofold breath <note>original: "Odem"; an archaic word for breath, specifically the divine breath of life.</note> into his nose, otherwise the image would have had no life or movement. However, neither the shadowy foundation of the completed human structure was meant to come to the fore, nor was the dark breath meant to *act* <note>original: "agiren"; to operate or exert influence.</note>; instead, both were to remain hidden in the *microcosmic* <term>microcosmic</term> <note>original: "microcosmischen"; referring to the philosophical concept that the human being is a "little world" mirroring the macrocosm of the entire universe.</note> depths. Just as with any other building, one should not see the foundation, but only the structure placed upon it. And if man, before the Fall, had not possessed the *potential* for mortality upon and within himself, he could not have reached for the mortal fruit or been led astray, and would thus have remained unchanged.

§. 110. ...and if we do not become wise through *Adam’s* and through our own fall, so that we understand the serpent’s cunning and learn to flee from it, then we fall yet another degree deeper than *Adam* placed us—namely, we fall out of the third *Principle* <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principio"; in the mystical philosophy of Jacob Boehme, the "Three Principles" describe the dark fire-world, the light-world of love, and the external physical world. Such an incorporated and concentrated light served him as an anchor, so that he could hold on to God, and by which the great *Jehovah* could also take hold of him again, so that he would not fall completely into Hell and become like *Lucifer*. Therefore *Moses* says in *Genesis 3:8* <note>original: "Mosis, Gen. III, 8."</note> that man, while he was still in the Garden of Paradise, heard and received the essential <term>essential</term> <note>original: "wesentliche"; here meaning a voice that carries the very substance or "essence" of God, rather than just an external sound.</note> voice of God. Such a divine voice was also a new covenant with God for him after the Fall, for the rebirth of both his soul and his body, verse 15. All those now who allow space and hearing in their souls for the essential inward-speaking <note>original: "Einsprache"; refers to God speaking directly into the inner spirit of a person.</note> of the divine light will, according to *1 Corinthians 3:14* <note>original: "1 Cor. 3, 14."</note>, receive a reward—that is, light; but the others will suffer eternal damage and loss: for the Cherub, as the divine fire, burns away and separates everything that is not born in us out of the light—that is, out of *Jesus*.

§. 119. That **Enoch and Elijah** <note>Two biblical figures said to have been taken directly to heaven by God without dying a natural death (Genesis 5:24; 2 Kings 2:11).</note> did not taste **temporal death** <note>Physical death that occurs within time, as opposed to eternal or spiritual death.</note> at all has no other cause than the great abundance of their spirit, which, in **conjunction** <term>Conjunction: a spiritual union or joining together of the human and divine.</term> with the great universal spirit of Jehovah, swallowed up the body and took it up with it; for the spirit is, as it were, the **wings** <note>original: "Geflügel"; here used metaphorically to mean the plumage or means of flight that allows the body to be lifted into the spiritual realm.</note> of the body. §. 121. In this corrupted **physical constitution** <note>The inherent makeup or health of the body.</note> now, Adam fathered children of his kind, and thus bequeathed death to all his descendants, 1 Corinthians 15:21. Consequently, Adam would have remained free from both sin and death. But since he nevertheless consented against the Spirit of God which forbade him in his soul (Genesis 3:3), he committed sin through that consent, and the actual eating brought him death, James 1:15. §. 122. Since we are all now begotten from such seed, in which a strong dose of the dark dust is contained, this very excess of such dust is **original sin** as a **matrix** <term>Matrix: In this context, a womb or breeding ground from which something originates.</term> for actual sin. Therefore, Christ and his apostles warn against gluttony and drunkenness, advising that one should indeed use food and drink for necessity, but not for luxury and lust, so that the dust is not **excited** <note>original: "excitiret"; stirred up or stimulated.</note>. For through their excessive use, our dust is increased to such an extent that its powers overflow in words and deeds. Just as daily experience teaches, those who tend to the body too much with all sorts of dainty foods and strong drinks commit various coarse **excesses** <note>original: "Excesse"; behavioral violations or lack of restraint.</note> and sins, which would not happen in a state of sobriety and moderation.

§. 124. Therefore, we must take good heed of the **original sin** <term>original sin: (German "Erbsünde") the innate tendency toward evil believed to be inherited from Adam's fall.</term> born into us all, so that we may prevent its activity and its outbreaks through the help of the divine light, so that after our **passing** <note>original: "Absterben"; death or the process of dying away from the world.</note>, the divine fire does not find so much to consume in our works and in our soul, and we must suffer great harm. 1 Cor. 3:15. <note>This biblical reference describes someone whose work is burned up but who is saved "only as through fire."</note>

§. 125. But although in this time we often **gain victory** <note>original: "victorisiren"; to win a specific battle or struggle.</note> over our internal **affects** <note>original: "Affecten"; powerful emotions or passions that disrupt spiritual peace.</note>, lusts, and desires dwelling in our dust, we cannot yet completely **triumph** <note>The author makes a distinction: "victory" is a successful battle, while "triumph" is the final, permanent celebration after the war is over.</note> over them until the conflict is at an end. As long as we still stand in the conflict, we may well speak of victory, but of no triumph; for original sin, which one day shall properly grant the triumph, still stirs within us and wants to **act** <note>original: "agiren"; to exert influence or take action.</note> 2 Cor. 12:7. <note>A reference to the "thorn in the flesh" that kept the Apostle Paul humble.</note> which then always causes us a new struggle, and this lasts until our life's end.

**Vocabulary:** Devil, babbler, original sin, divine light, passing, divine fire, soul, affects, lusts, desires, victory, triumph, struggle, life's end.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 83 concerning the spiritual struggle within the "dust" of our physical nature. §. 126. When the soul, as our **active agent** <term>active agent: (Latin "Agens") the driving force or inner actor that directs a person's life.</term>, departs from the body, the influence of the "dust" <note>The "dust" refers to the corruptible, earthly nature of man mentioned in previous sections.</note> ceases; for the soul is, as it were, a **double channel**, through which either the eternal light or the eternal darkness enters us and carries out its work through the body. Positioned above this double channel is the **Will**, which must always provide the deciding factor. Whichever of the two eternal principles the Will opens itself to, that one enters and becomes active. Consequently, the effects of the eternal light bring nothing but **bliss** <note>original: "Seligkeit"; a state of eternal happiness or salvation.</note>, while the effects of the eternal darkness bring nothing but damnation and harm. The former shall remain, but the latter shall be consumed in the **divine fire**. 1 Corinthians 3; Deuteronomy 4:24. <note>Deuteronomy 4:24 describes God as a "consuming fire." 1 Corinthians 3:15 speaks of works being burned while the person is saved "as through fire."</note> If such a burning does not happen during this lifetime, it will certainly happen in the next life, and indeed much more painfully and over a longer duration, as all the saints—of both the Old and New Testaments—have clearly recognized in the light of God and have experienced in their own souls during this life.

§. 127. Therefore, they were also prompted to establish a **cleansing of the soul** or a **purgatory** <note>original: "Fegfeuer"; literally "cleansing fire."</note> after death. This chief and fundamental article of faith has indeed been preserved by the **Roman Church** until now, though it is quite wrongly understood and poorly practiced by most. The **Protestants**, on the other hand, have entirely rejected such an excellent and highly necessary article, and in its place have set a well-founded but wrongly applied **satisfaction** <term>satisfaction: (German "Genugthuung") the doctrine that Christ’s suffering paid the debt for human sin, often used by Protestants to argue against the need for purgatorial cleansing.</term>, by which the...

<meta>catchword: most- (from "meisten")</meta>

§. 128. §. 135. In such a resurrection, those who belong to Him then follow; that is, those who have crucified their flesh together with its lusts and desires, and have also become strong in the Spirit and similar to His image. ### §. 140.
Others extend the **merit of Christ** even over all **actual and gross sins** <term>würkliche und grobe Sünden: In theology, "actual" sins are specific individual acts of wrongdoing, such as lying or theft, as opposed to "original" or "inherited" sin.</term>; by this, they make people entirely secure in all their malice, as is clear as day. ### §. 143.

Satisfaction <note>original: *Genugthuung*. In a 17th-century theological context, this refers to the doctrine of "penal substitutionary atonement," or the idea that Christ's suffering paid a legal debt for human sin.</note> [which would] take souls magically filled with sins and filth into Paradise, and see those same [souls] eternally before Him under the **satisfaction-cover** <term>Satisfaction-cover: a polemical term (similar to *Sündendeckel*) used by mystical writers to mock the idea that Christ's merits simply "cover" a person's sins like a lid, without actually changing their sinful nature.</term>—a thing that is not even to be thought of, much less said, regarding His most holy Majesty. Christ has indeed become a model for our souls, so that we should walk and act as He acted and walked, but not a **sin-cover**. All those who deny this, or even impugn <note>original: *impugniren*; to attack, dispute, or call into question.</note> it, clearly show that they still love actual sin for the preservation of their **old Adam-like nature** <term>Old Adam: a common theological term for the fallen, unregenerate state of humanity that must be "put off" to become a new person in Christ.</term> far more than the following of Christ and the denial of themselves, and that they are far from being minded to become God's genuine children.

§. 155. The following <note>original: *Nachfolge*; often referring to the "Imitation of Christ."</note> so strongly recommended to us by Christ and His apostles, in the denial of the world and of our own lives, clearly shows that there is no satisfaction for the soul and for actual sin; for if it were so, that the death and resurrection of Christ provided satisfaction <note>original: *satisfacirete*; from the Latin *satisfacere*, meaning to pay a debt or fulfill an obligation.</note> for actual sins, and yet denial on our part were nonetheless commanded, then either the denial would have to be superfluous and unnecessary, or the satisfaction would not be sufficient—both of which would be absurd and would diminish the merit of Christ. We would also in such a way

<summary>The author critiques the idea that Christ's atonement acts as a "cover" for sin without requiring personal transformation. He argues that if legal satisfaction were enough, the biblical commands for self-denial and following Christ's example would be pointless.</summary>

...never understand Moses <note>original: *Mosen*, the Latinized accusative form.</note> nor the Messiah <note>original: *Messiam*.</note> and his apostles, and the Scripture would always remain a sealed book to us. §. 156. For 1) according to the spirit, he is himself the great Jehovah, who made heaven and earth, and even to this day creates, maintains, and bears all things. This spirit is but one in all things. Through and in such a spirit, he and his apostles performed all wonders and signs, etc. etc.

§. 157. 2) According to the soul, he was a pure and holy angel <note>original: *Engel*. In this mystical tradition, the author likely views Christ's soul as a "celestial" or "angelic" substance, the archetype of human perfection, rather than just a created spirit.</note>, who was at all times subject and obedient to the Spirit. In this subjection and obedience, he shone as a light before all people and showed us—more through his example <note>original: *Exempel*.</note> than with words—how we, following him, must seek to become like him in this regard, if we do not wish to suffer eternal harm. His conduct, however, consisted in the practice of love for one's neighbor and in a constant watchfulness and oversight of all stirrings and of all his temptations and enemies, both inward and outward, which sought to bring him to a fall, just as they did the first Adam <term>First Adam: the biblical figure from Genesis, representing fallen human nature that succumbed to temptation.</term>.

§. 158. 3) According to the body, he was a human being just like us, surrounded on all sides by frailty <note>original: *Schwachheit*; in this context, it refers to the physical limitations and vulnerabilities of the human condition</note>, yet without any actual sin (Hebrews 4:15). These frailties came to him from nothing other than the original sin <note>original: *Erbsünde*</note> he took in and upon himself, which he bore for our sake so that he might heal the damage caused by Adam. I have already sufficiently proven this above with many arguments <note>original: *Argumentis*</note> and reasons <note>original: *Rationibus*</note>, and I add to the conclusion of this little work: that Christ, the Savior <note>original: *Heyland*</note> of the whole world, had to make the beginning within himself in order to conquer <term>conquer|Ueberwindung: This refers to a spiritual victory or "overcoming" of the lower nature and spiritual enemies</term> sin, death, the devil, and hell. For how else could he have directed us toward the following <note>discipleship or imitation of Christ</note> and conquest of such mighty enemies if he had not gone before us and conquered them himself (Revelation 3:21; Luke 9:23). And how could he, as a true human being, know that such conquest is so difficult, and thus have compassion for our weakness, if he did not know it from practice <note>original: *Praxi*</note> and experience? Therefore, he now gives us of his Spirit and supports our weakness, so that we too can conquer just as he has conquered. And since we wish to be called followers and members of his body, we must not let our Head <note>Christ is traditionally described as the "Head" of the church, which is his "Body"</note> work and struggle alone; rather, we must also do our part and must always begin the conquest within ourselves and not in others.

§. 159. The Principle of Death, into which the eternal darkness has an entrance, caused the Savior many a struggle and many a temptation, both inward and outward; but he always overcame, constantly crushing the head of the serpent <note>A reference to the biblical prophecy in Genesis 3:15, where the offspring of the woman would crush the head of the serpent (symbolizing evil or the devil).</note> within himself, and never consented to the temptations, much less allowed them to break out into action. For this reason, the realm of the dead could not hold him, because his spirit was more powerful than his body and he had not sinned; and he has herewith become a cause of eternal salvation <note>original: *Seligkeit*; refers to a state of spiritual blessedness or ultimate happiness in the afterlife</note> for all his followers; just as, conversely, Adam had become a cause of death and eternal damnation for all his followers.

§. 160. If Christ had not taken upon himself the very dust <note>A biblical metaphor for human mortality and the physical body, referring to Genesis where man is created from the dust of the ground.</note> which brings death to all people, he could neither have died nor been killed; consequently, he could not have made a beginning toward the complete conquest of sin, death, and hell. If that were so, our bodies would have remained in death forever, and the soul would have had no dwelling place in eternity. Adam made the beginning of sin and death within himself and brought this misfortune upon the entire human race (Romans 5:12). Christ, however, made the beginning of the abolition of these enemies within himself, and thereby brought salvation to all people; and because he [gave] his innocent life

§. 161. When he was put to death and laid in the grave, he did not experience decay <note>original: *Verwesung*; the physical process of decomposition</note>, and left behind no "dead earth" of his corpse as we do. For without Christ and his resurrection, no one could have obtained a triumph or a sign of victory, because death was too powerful.

§. 162. §. 166. p. 5. <term>Art</term> <note>original: *Die Kunst*; refers to the practice of alchemy or the skill of the alchemist</note> cannot create or make the proper pieces in the fundamental beginning: It is already made by nature, whom the artist must now only assist, for nature cannot separate the impure from the pure in the earth as quickly as the artist does in this work; the stinking <term>sulfur</term> <note>original: *Sulphur*; in alchemy, this refers to a combustible principle of matter, often considered an impurity in its "stinking" or unrefined state</note> must be separated from the core.

§. 167. p. 6. The <term>subject</term> <note>original: *Subjectum*</note>, the matter or <term>impure chaos</term> <note>original: *Chaos impurum*; the raw, unrefined starting material of the Great Work</note>, which nature has brought into a metallic form in the earth but left imperfect—this same material the artist must gradually purify without excessive heat. For if it has too much heat, the spirit of life flies away and leaves its body lying dead; if it has too little heat, it will not move toward life or growth. For the internal essence is a pure, fiery, **sulfurous**, incombustible being which, once it is <term>fixed</term> <note>original: *fix*; made stable and resistant to evaporation when heated</note>, might be called the **Light of Nature**; for it is the brilliance and form of all metals, which illuminates and perfects all bodies. §. 170. p. 18. Unless you know how to make the bodies spiritual, and the spirits bodily, you have not yet found the right way or process for this venerable Art; which is nothing other than making the thick thin and light, and the light thick and heavy, as *Hermes* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note> says: *make the thick subtle and return this as thick* <note>original Latin: *crassum fac subtile et hoc spissum reddito*</note>, and this is because everything thin and light is considered more worthy than the coarse and thick. For heavy things cannot rise to the heights unless they are first united with light things; and conversely, light things cannot be **fixed** <note>original: *figirt*; rendered stable and resistant to fire</note> or kept at the bottom, except through the strength and power of the heavy, coarse, and thick parts. However, so that they may both act and be acted upon by one another, the **fixed** and the **volatile** <note>original: *fixe und flüchtige*; substances that resist heat versus those that evaporate</note> must be properly combined according to the **weight** <note>original: *pondere*</note> of the Sages, and mixed like water with water, so that even the force of fire is unable to separate them.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the composition of metals, moving from the physical processes of heat to the philosophical understanding of the "First Matter" and the elemental composition (Sulfur and Mercury) of all metallic bodies. It maintains the internal reference system (page numbers and section markers) common in early modern alchemical compendiums.</meta>

§. 171. pag. 20. The **First Matter** <note>original: *erste Materie*; the *Materia Prima* or the fundamental substance from which all things are created</note> of all metals is entirely the same in power and virtue; the difference, however, consists only in their purification and **digestion** <note>original: *Digestion*; the process of ripening or maturing a substance through gentle, steady heat</note>, and that one is more **digested** and cooked by Nature than the other. Through the degrees of **preparation**, however, all **accidents** <note>original: *Zufälle*; in Aristotelian philosophy, these are non-essential qualities (like color or hardness) that can change without changing the essence of the substance</note> and accidental parts that contaminate the lower metals must be separated from them, so that alone their pure, incorruptible substance remains. Our **Mercury** <term>Mercurius; not common quicksilver, but the "philosophical" liquid principle representing the spirit or soul of the matter</term> is the earth; what one sows into it, that it brings forth. *Mercury embraces both natures* <note>original Latin: *Mercurius amplectitur utramque naturam*; referring to Mercury's dual nature as both fixed and volatile, or male and female</note>.

§. 172. p. 26. Metals do not differ from one another, except solely for the sake of accidental effects; that is, that one has received a purer or unpurer **Sulfur** <term>Sulphur; in alchemy, the active, oily, and combustible principle representing the "body" or "fire" of a metal</term> and **Mercury**, a more tempered or untempered heat, or a combustible or incombustible Sulfur in the womb of the earth. The **Philosophers** <note>the alchemists of old</note> have associated the lower bodies <note>the metals</note> with those that stand above in the firmament <note>the planets</note>, and it was right for them to do so.

§. 173. pag. 28. You should know that Nature has its passages and its veins in the earth, which drink, lick, drip, and yield salty, clear, and unclear waters, as can be seen in the mines. If now the salty

water penetrates from above, for all heavy things sink toward the depths; these are met by the sulfurous vapors rising from below **from the center of the earth** <note>original Latin: *ex centro terrae*</note>. If it is found that the saline waters are pure and clear, and the sulfurous vapors are also pure, and they meet and encounter one another, then a good metal is produced. If not, an impure metal results, upon which Nature must work for a thousand years, more or less, before she makes it perfect; and this occurs because of the impurity of either the **mercurial** <term>mercurialisch; relating to the fluid, spiritual principle of Mercury</term> saline waters or the impure sulfurous vapor. When these two receive one another in a closed cleft or stone, a moist, thick, fatty vapor rises from them both, through the actual heat of Nature. ...momentarily tinge **Venus** <note>original: *Venerem*; the alchemical name for copper</note> into apparent **Gold** <note>original: *Sole*; the alchemical name for gold</note>, but it is not permanent; yet from this one sees the possibility. ### §. 176. page 39. 
When metals have lost their **Tinging Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritum tingentem*; the transformative essence that imparts color or quality</note> in the fire, they are **alone** <note>original: *sola*</note> and dead. Body, soul, and spirit are no longer together, for the spirit has left its body and the soul is also powerless; it is therefore impossible to make anything from them. Bernard <note>referring to Bernard of Trevisan, a 15th-century alchemist often cited in later texts</note> rejects all minerals that do not bear metals, as well as the metals themselves when they have been separated from their life by the fire. 

**Page 41.** The life of metals flees from the common metals in the fire; they are then dead, since their spiritual essence or **Tincture** <term>Tincture; in alchemy, a potent substance or "medicine" capable of transmuting and "coloring" base metals into noble ones</term>, which is meant to perform the tinging, is gone. For one body cannot penetrate or tinge another body; the spirit is the driver, it is he who must do it.

### §. 177. page 42.

### §. 178. page 43. 
When the Mercury of the metals is first only slightly congealed or solidified, there is soon present within it a constant grain of **Gold**, which—from the two seeds, **fatty sulfur and salt** <note>original: *sulphure pingui & sale*; these were considered the fundamental "seeds" or components of all metals in alchemical theory</note>—produces and begets a true little sprout of our Mercury. ### §. 179. page 50. 
We should and must take the very materials from which Nature makes metals in the earth. **Bernhard** <note>Bernhardus Trevisanus (1406–1490), a famous alchemist who claimed that the "Stone" must be made from a single metallic root</note> says that Nature first makes a leaden matter; in this, she has placed her first **inclination** <note>original: *Disposition*</note> and property, and through this, one can come closest to the goal. The other bodies, such as **gold** and **silver** <note>original: *Sol* (the Sun) and *Luna* (the Moon)</note> etc., are too hard to break open; therefore, we call them a **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> in which there is exactly such a pure **sulfur and mercury** <note>the two fundamental components of all metals in alchemical theory</note> as in silver and gold, but in which Nature has only worked a little and has left it imperfect. 

**Page 52.** In our lead, before it touches the fire, there is **gold and silver in potential** <note>original: *potentia*</note>, even if one cannot see it while it is joined together. But when its spirit separates from the body, it leaves behind the **lunar body** <note>original: *Corpus lunare*; silver-like matter</note>, which appears as the most beautiful silver in all tests. 

**Page 53.** The **Assembly of Philosophers** <note>original: *Turba*, referring to the *Turba Philosophorum*, a foundational alchemical text</note> says: that in **Saturn** <note>the alchemical name for lead</note>, which has not yet been through the fire, there is always hidden a grain or several of **gold or silver**, like a seed that can be propagated. ## §. 181. page 59. 
All metals, the pure as well as the impure, are inwardly **Gold** <term>Sol</term>, **Silver** <term>Luna</term>, and **Mercury** <term>Mercurius</term>. It is well worth considering that a metallic and **Saturnian body** <note>original: *saturnisch Corpus*; referring to lead, as Saturn is the planetary correspondence for lead</note> exists which one can easily **dissolve** <term>solviren</term> and **putrefy** <term>putrificiren</term>; whoever knows its correct preparation may rightly rejoice. **The Assembly** <note>original: *Turba*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts</note> says: I tell you that our sulfurous, living water is drawn out from **lead ore** <note>original: *Bleyerz*</note>, which accomplishes everything. **Likewise** <note>original: *Item*</note>, you should know that the spirit of silver and all whiteness must be composed from the lead ore in such a way that the **Silver** <term>Luna</term> is a white stone (**salt of metals** <note>original: *sal metallorum*</note>), without any blemish or impurity. 

The whiteness that shines there is drawn from the **Saturnian body** <note>the physical substance of lead</note>; it is beautifully clear, and one sees no **Silver** <term>Luna</term> in it. But when it is thrown onto a hot plate of **Copper** <term>Venus</term> or **Iron** <term>Mars</term>, it melts like wax, and the spirit of life flies away above, leaving the **Lunar essence** <term>Lunariam</term> lying beautifully behind it. For this **white Silver** <note>original: *alba Luna*</note> does not look like common silver, for it is transparent. 

### Page 60. 
**Paracelsus's** <note>original: *Theophrasti*; referring to Theophrastus von Hohenheim, known as Paracelsus</note> **unripe mineral alloy** <term>electum minerale immaturum</term> is such a thing in which nature has planted the properties of all seven metals but left them imperfect; therefore, he calls it **unripe** <term>immaturum</term>.

<summary>This page discusses the alchemical extraction of a "spiritual" silver from lead ore, describing a process where a transparent, wax-like essence is separated from the base metal. It also introduces Paracelsus's concept of an "unripe" mineral containing the potential of all seven metals.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of mineral sources for lead and silver, specifically identifying high-quality ores from various mining regions. §. 182. page 64. The most immediate material is either found at **Villach** <note>a historic mining city in Carinthia, Austria, known for its lead deposits</note>, a quite native **Lead** <note>original: *Bley*</note> that has never been in a fire; this is easily dissolved and contains its **silver essence** <term>Lunariam</term> abundantly within it, and is thus marked †♁ <note>The symbols for a cross and the *globus cruciger*; in this context, it likely designates a specific purified state of the mineral or its "earthly" nature</note>. Or, what is even better, is found in **Meissen** <note>a region in Saxony, Germany</note>, though it is rarely found at this time; however, if one orders it, it can be obtained. And there they call it **silver glance** <term>Glaserz</term> <note>a high-grade silver ore, technically argentite</note>; it is like lead, can be cut and stamped <note>meaning it is malleable enough to be impressed with a seal or coin die</note>, and a hundredweight often yields 24 to 26 **lots** <term>loth</term> <note>a traditional German unit of weight, roughly 1/32nd of a pound</note> of **Silver**; from one pound of it, I have obtained 22 lots of **Mercury** <term>Mercurius</term>. There is also silver glance at **St. Annaberg** <note>a famous mining town in the Ore Mountains of Saxony</note>, which is also rich in silver, indistinguishable from other lead, can be stamped and cut, and is good for dissolving.

...and Earth were created, it was all only one water, which was the <term>first matter</term> <note>original: *prima Materia*; the primordial substance from which all things were formed according to both Genesis and alchemical theory</note>, within which Heaven and Earth with their whole host, along with the abyss of the Earth—so encompassed with darkness, empty, without strength and life—were submerged and contained. But the abyss of the Earth and the darkness, as the useless, dead part, He abandoned, drawing only the powers of Light and Life from the Earth into the water, so that right at the beginning, through the Spirit of God, a division occurred within this <term>dissolution</term> <note>original: *Solution*; the process of breaking a solid down into liquid, considered the first step in alchemical transformation</note> and <term>decay</term> <note>original: *Putrefaction*; the breakdown of matter to release its inner spirit</note>.
